# X.KF.W2 - Parse Façade

**Wave**: KF.W2 · Parse Façade — one kf-side `parse()` façade collapsing the failure postures over the Tier-A grammar seam; the six Tier-C ad-hoc regexes deleted including the `cssom.ts` identifier injection; the entry-point contract and the R1 ingress census published once. **This wave is the pre-work that makes KF.W3's parser consumption one-touch.** Its product is a *surface plus two registries* (postures, ingresses), not a behaviour change.
**Half**: library (keyframes.js `src/`), with a declared demo-side census arm. **Sub-tranche**: X·KF (SS-1 library half), authority `docs/tranches/X/COHESION.md` §0/§1, ratified under `docs/tranches/V/megatranche/SCOPE.md` **M-25**. Validated at authoring against `docs/precepts/instructions/tranche/WAVE_SPEC.md` (**L-20**).
**Status**: **planned**. No product byte is written by this file; authoring it opened no product source for writing. Execution awaits the owner's begin-word.
**Alias law (R-B idiom)**: `KF.W2` denotes `X.KF.W2`; `X.KF.W2.<x>` is the WAVE_SPEC §5 unit form. Search alias, never a rename. Spec home: `docs/tranches/X/keyframes/waves/KF-W2.md` — the in-tree sibling convention (KF-W4.md, KF-W7.md). The drafting seat's `docs/tranches/X/kf/waves/KF.W2.md` target came from an artifact that **does not exist in-tree** and is struck (§Provenance; RULINGS **R-12**). No file is re-glyphed.

## Goal criterion

This wave succeeds if, when work ends, **there is exactly one module in `src/animation/**` that speaks to value.js's grammar, exactly one declared thing that happens when a CSS string is bad, and one published answer to "which grammar belongs at this seam"** — so that KF.W3's repin is an edit to one file, and so that no later seat has to re-discover, for the fourth time, which value.js entry throws on what. A wave that adds a wrapper and leaves the twenty-one call sites and the **nineteen enumerated postures** standing has failed this goal. (The integer is the registry's **floor**, and the floor is the **enumeration at §Carry F3 / G-W2-1** — this sentence points at that enumeration and does not re-issue its numeral (**LAW D(3)**, repair round 4); **carriage per `docs/tranches/X/keyframes/conformance/PASS-4/KF-W2-CHECK.md` §2 (2026-08-28)**, which found seven unenumerated cells and named them **Z1–Z7** — all seven booked at repair round 4, **one of them a posture** (`KF-KE-2`, F3 row 19, which is why the floor reads nineteen and not eighteen). The charter's "five", the drafting seat's "eight", round 1's "eleven" and round 2's "sixteen" were each refuted by the bank: the first three on **arithmetic and basis**, the fourth on **instrument** — round 2's basis was the whole 58 and its exclusions were still established by a token count that returns zero on the very cells it had adopted. **The floor is a floor; its basis is in the gate; and from round 3 an exclusion resting on a token-match alone reds the gate.**)

**Execution shape**: 4 Opus seats, 3 phases — phase 1: 1 serial (`.a`, the census + reconciliation + posture registry: the two registries size everything after them); phase 2: 2 parallel, file-disjoint (`.b` façade + Tier-A repoint; `.c` Tier-C deletion + the cssom cure); phase 3: 1 serial (`.d`, the round-trip net, the fuzz corpus extension, the §F-2 addendum, close). Peak concurrency 2 — inside the owner's four-workflow cap.

**Why this block exists.** LAW A binds every seat that *writes, carries, or leaves standing* an act that deletes, repoints or shims a module — and this wave carries four such acts plus one repoint family. Docs/prose hits are recorded as non-import context and never counted. **Two of the five refute a figure this file previously carried.**

## §Carry

Every routed registry cell lands as a gate, a bounds item, a census cell, a named cure with its locks, or an §Excluded line **with a named owner**. Deduped by identity: **the R1 crash books ONCE at megatranche R1** and is re-booked by no component and no gate here. Severity is the bank's. Ids are the bank's — no row carried here is renamed (anti-rename).

**Census accounting (repair round 1; R-12 — the phantom "11 rows, 11 carried, 0 dropped" is struck)**: **28** cells were carried at authoring; **19** escaped and were adopted at round 1 — RULINGS **R-1b**'s four (kf-DemoGlobalChrome **C-10** · kf-SquareScene **C S-C** · kf-KeyboardShortcutsModal **C-§1/R-B** · kf-SpringTrace **D-12 = L-6 = C-4**), the kf-KeyframesStringControls posture pair (**C-2** · **D-6/L-BL-3/C-1**), the **nine** further R1 verified negatives, the two easing/CSS-organ cells (**KF-ET-2** · **KF-TFP-20**), kf-CubeScene's **C-L-2 residual**, and kf-SquareScene **D-27/L-7/C-9**'s crash-surface annotation. **4 + 2 + 9 + 2 + 1 + 1 = 19; 28 + 19 = 47.** *Arithmetic note (so the two counts cannot be read as a discrepancy)*: F0's round-1 boundary table holds **eleven** rows, of which **two** — kf-DemoGlobalChrome **C-10** and kf-KeyboardShortcutsModal **C-§1/R-B** — are R-1b's and are counted in the four; the nine are the remainder. - **KF-KE-3** ⟨kf-KeyframesEditor; seam located by kf-KeyframeCardList⟩ **BLOCKER** — the start field validates through the **wrong entry point**: `parseCssScalar` (any CSS scalar) instead of the keyframe grammar. Verbatim: *"admits 500%/-20%, renders them, then fails ~1 s later as 'Could not update keyframes' from a different op naming neither field nor stop; rejects from/to that the sibling add path accepts via `requireKeyframeSelector` … Frontier-durable (`parseCssScalar` at frontier `:186`). → KFED-UNIT (`requireKeyframeSelector` at the field; the S-5/C-S5 error posture stays — it is the reference implementation asking the wrong function)."*
  **RE-EXECUTED CONFIRMATION + one precision correction** (this seat, 2026-08-28): `parseCssScalar("500%")` → `ok:true {number,500,%}`; `("-20%")` → `ok:true`; `parseKeyframeSelector("500%")` / `("-20%")` → `ok:false [keyframe_selector_invalid]`. The banked admissions are exact. **Correction**: `parseCssScalar("from")` → **`ok:true`** (keyword payload; `"to"` likewise) — the entry does **not** reject `from`/`to`; the *field* fails downstream. A cure written against "`parseCssScalar` rejects `from`" is written against a false mechanism. Also measured: the selector grammar returns a **fraction** (`"from"|"to"|"50%"` → `{kind:"percent", value: 0|1|0.5}`) where the scalar returns `500`/`-20`/`50` — the unit mismatch that MISS-β2's `*100` exists to bridge, and the source of its corruption set.
  **Second precision correction**: the reference implementation is **`demo/utils/keyframeSelector.ts:14-15`** (`requireKeyframeSelector`, verified at origin/master) — `useKeyframeOps.ts:174` is its **call site**. It matters: the correct-grammar wrapper lives in the *same file* as serializer copy 3, so MISS-β2's publication decision and KF-KE-3's cure share a file. → **G-W2-6** (contract, born-RED). **KF.W2 does not open the call site.**

Cured at repair round 2 by **G-W2-2b**, the paired demo clause, whose falsifier arms on the same terms as G-W2-2's. Pairs with KF-CE-12's depcruise-over-demo arm at KF.W4. Prose cure is KFED-UNIT's; E-3 — the false comment is corrected in the cure commit, never rewritten in the dated record.

  **Y8 · C-2 IS NEVER CITED WITHOUT ITS KILL QUALIFIER AGAIN — adopted at repair round 3 (RULINGS-3 §END · KF-W2-CHECK D-15; M-25).** This file cited C-2 at **two** load-bearing sites (here and §Excluded) while the sibling record **kills C-2's exculpation at the bytes**, and the kill **strengthens this wave's own thesis**. Carried verbatim, both limbs, with their ids: **kf-SpringTarget ruling 12 / K-7** ⟨`:30`, `:93`⟩ — *"C-2's exculpation (**'keyframes.js publishes no numeric spring sampler; the parser is forced'**) + its §5.3 library prescription — **KILLED at the bytes**: `springTimingFunction(opts): Easing` is barrel-exported; `timing-function.ts:91-100` builds `.fn` from the SAME `sampleNormalizedSpring` with the same `response * 4` default, `fn(0)=0`/`fn(1)=1` pinned (`:83-89`). **DECISIVE and unseen by all three axes: the demo already consumes `.fn` as a numeric sampler in this very directory** — `useSpringKeyframesEditor.ts:44-52` … The parser contradicts the directory's own idiom, which **STRENGTHENS M-4**; publishing `sampleNormalizedSpring` (C §5.3) is an unnecessary library change. **C-2's endpoint arithmetic survives inside M-3.**"* And the mechanism half, **M-4** ⟨`:45`, NO-WAVE-OWNER⟩ — *"**SpringTrace round-trips the engine through CSS text** (`springLinearStops` string → **~40-line regex reparse**, `SpringTrace.vue:50-86`) when the engine's public `springTimingFunction(...).fn` hands it the same curve numerically … The swap deletes M-3 and both dead guards; `.css` still supplies the stop-count label."*
  **What this changes in this wave's argument, stated exactly** (it is a strengthening, not a widening of scope): C-2 was cited here as evidence that the Tier-C serialize→regex-reparse class is **wider** than the six `src/` sites. With the kill attached it becomes evidence that the class is **wider AND ELECTIVE** — the sanctioned numeric route is already published, already barrel-exported, and **already consumed three files away** — which is the strongest available statement of the anti-pattern the façade exists to make visible, and the exact converse of an *"the parser is forced"* defence a later seat might otherwise inherit from C-2's bare text. **Nothing else moves**: M-4/K-7 stay **NO-WAVE-OWNER**, the SpringTrace cure travels with the spring packet to **KF.W11** (§Sequencing), and this wave gates none of it — G-W2-3 remains scoped to the six named `src/` sites, because widening it to cover an unowned row would make that row this wave's blocker (§Excluded).

**G-W2-2 — Tier-A single-entry (`src/` arm).** RED — **21** parse-surface call sites over **10** runtime importer modules at the ref of record.

*Why this gate was re-cut at repair round 2 (RULINGS-2 R2-6.1 · KF-W2-CHECK D-1) — three independent faults, each fatal, each reproduced by the round-2 seat before the re-cut was written*:

```
$ git grep -nE "import[^;]*\b(parseStylesheet|…|collect(…))\b" 81a56990 -- src/     → 0 hits          (1)
$ git grep -nP "import[^;]*\b(parseStylesheet|…|collect(…))\b" 81a56990 -- src/     → 5 hits, 5 files (2,3)
    src/animation/compile/frame/compiler.ts:40   ← kf's OWN parseKeyframeSelector, from "../selector"
    src/animation/compile/value/compile.ts:1 · engine/options.ts:17 · resolve/browser.ts:3 · validate.ts:47
```

1. **Inert.** POSIX ERE defines no `\b`; git grep's `-E` engine matched nothing. **A gate asserting 21 sites was witnessed by a command naming none, at the ref the spec pins** — the exact failure this wave's own doctrine (a phantom coordinate convicts) exists to catch.
2. **Line-oriented against multi-line imports.** Even repaired to `-P` it names **4** of the 10 parse-surface modules and is blind to `adapter.ts` (block `:1-14`), `easing/registry.ts` (`:11-15`), `emit/format/options.ts` (`:24-28`), `selector.ts` (`:2-6`), `engine/css/metadata.ts` (`:22-30`), `scroll/grammar.ts` (`:32-44`). A seat could route the four single-line importers through the façade and the check would read *"0 modules"* with six runtime grammar importers still standing.
3. **Self-conflating.** `frame/compiler.ts:40` — `import { FRAME_ID_SCALE, parseKeyframeSelector } from "../selector";` — is **kf's own** wrapper, the precise conflation §Carry F1 corrects for two of the banked thirteen. The gate imported the bug it was written to fix.

*Witness (re-cut, TWO commands, each re-executed at `81a56990` this round with its output stated)*:

**(i) Module enumeration — specifier-anchored, so kf's own symbols cannot enter.**
```
$ git grep -n 'from "@mkbabb/value.js/css"' 81a56990 -- src/ | wc -l   → 29 specifier lines
$ git grep -l 'from "@mkbabb/value.js/css"' 81a56990 -- src/ | wc -l   → 27 files
```
**(ii) Per-file runtime-symbol read over exactly those files — multi-line tolerant by construction (it reads whole import blocks, not lines), and `type`-filtered.**
```
$ for f in $(git grep -l 'from "@mkbabb/value.js/css"' 81a56990 -- src/ | sed 's|^81a56990:||'); do
    git show 81a56990:$f | perl -0777 -ne 'BEGIN{$F=shift @ARGV}
      while(/import\s+(type\s+)?\{([^}]*)\}\s*from\s*"\@mkbabb\/value\.js\/css"/gs){
        next if $1; for my $m (split /,/,$2){ $m=~s/^\s+|\s+$//g; next unless length $m;
        next if $m=~/^type\b/; print "$F :: $m\n" } }' "$f"; done
  → 25 runtime specifiers over 13 modules
```
**Stated reading, reproduced**: **13** runtime importer modules — **10 on the parse surface** (`adapter` ×6 · `easing/registry` ×1 · `emit/format/options` ×1 · `selector` ×1 · `value/compile` ×1 · `engine/css/metadata` ×2 · `engine/options` ×1 · `resolve/browser` ×1 · `scroll/grammar` ×6 · `validate` ×2 = **22 grammar/collector specifiers**), **2 emit-half** (`emit/css-text.ts:8` · `frame/interp-slot.ts:8`, `serializeCssColor` — Tier-D, out of denominator), **1 stray** (`resolve/function.ts` `coerceToSyntax`). Against those 13 sit the **21 call sites** enumerated in §Carry F1 with their lines.

**Each of the three faults dies structurally, not by patching the regex**: (1) the commands carry no `\b` and no `-E`, so there is nothing to be inert about; (2) command (ii) reads **whole import blocks** across newlines, so no multi-line importer can hide; (3) **both commands are anchored on the specifier `"@mkbabb/value.js/css"`**, so `frame/compiler.ts:40`'s `from "../selector"` cannot match — **kf's own `parseKeyframeSelector` is excluded by construction, not by a name on a deny-list.** The one place the two names legitimately meet is `compile/selector.ts`, which imports the **package's** `parseKeyframeSelector` **aliased to `parseValueSelector`** (`:2-6`, read) — a real value.js entry, correctly counted; the aliasing is what made the conflation possible and is now stated in the gate.

*Census corrections carried*: two of the banked thirteen (`adapter.ts:266`, `engine/css/animation.ts:176`) name kf's **own** `resolveKeyframes`; `frame/compiler.ts:146` is the unnamed Tier-A-adjacent consumer; `resolve/function.ts`'s `coerceToSyntax` is the stray runtime import, delete-or-declare at open.
*Assertion*: **exactly one module in `src/animation/**` imports value.js's grammar entries or collectors at runtime** — command (ii) returns the parse-surface symbols at **exactly one path, `parse-facade.ts`**, and command (i)'s file count may still exceed 1 (the type surface and the emit half legitimately stand). **21 sites → 1 module.**
*Falsifier*: a second runtime import edge of any grammar/collector symbol anywhere under `src/animation/**` reds it — and, because command (ii) enumerates by file, **a repair that removes an importer without routing its call sites reds too** (the sites in §Carry F1 are the second half of the denominator). Out of scope, explicitly: the emit-half `serializeCssColor` imports (Tier-D, §4.4 lockstep, moved at W3/W8) and the type-only surface — the façade collapses *behaviour*, not types (27 `src/` files name the subpath, most type-only; 2 of the 15 lexically-runtime statements are inline-`type` with no runtime edge; collapsing any of that is a different, unmandated act). **And, declared at repair round 3 (RULINGS-3 LAW A; §Bounds census A-5): the `test/` arm — 10 specifier lines over 10 files, `scripts/` 0.** Until this round this gate was `src/`-scoped and G-W2-2b was `demo/`-scoped, and **nothing in this wave had ever looked at `test/`**, which is a quadrant of the same import graph and holds **`test/compile/valuejs-contract.test.ts:1` — a file this wave MODIFIES.** The ten are **censused, frozen, and explicitly NOT a cure surface**: a conformance suite that reaches the library's own façade to test the library's own façade proves nothing, so the tests keep their direct grammar edges by design. What changes is that this gate's *"exactly one module in `src/animation/**`"* is now asserted against a graph whose remaining quadrant is **known** rather than unlooked-at — the KF-KE-54 breach (*"the gate guarding the façade must not be `src/`-scoped"*) reached further than the demo clause that cured it.

## §Sequencing

**Hard ordering (blocking, in order):**
1. **KF.W0 §B-12 substrate settle** (OP-2). The wave's opening commit **states its ref** — `origin/master 81a56990` or later — and no anchor is trusted before it. The §Bounds drift table is this wave's contribution to that settle and should be consumed by KF.W0's re-anchor pass rather than re-derived.
2. **Phase 1 (`.a`) publishes both registries before any cure commit.** The posture registry (G-W2-1) and the ingress census (G-W2-5) size everything after them, and **KF.W7 may not decide a failure posture before KF.W2 publishes the registry** (declared at both ends — KF-W7.md G14 resolves against this registry, verified).
3. **The façade exists before any Tier-A repoint and before any Tier-C deletion** — a deletion without its replacement call is a regression (G-W2-3's falsifier).
4. **`emit/format/format.ts` is written by `.c` only** (shared file; §Bounds disjointness).
5. **G-W2-6 publishes the contract; KFED-UNIT edits the call site** — after KF.W0 and after KF.W4's vue-tsc gate, because *"nothing in the template layer is verifiable until a `.vue` file can fail a build"* (KF-CE-16, the sequencing head).
6. **No repin.** OP-5 — the façade must be green against installed 4.0.0 at close, or KF.W3 inherits an unmeasurable delta.

**Commit families (rows that must not split):** the façade + its first Tier-A repoint (one commit — a façade with zero callers is not a seam); each Tier-C deletion **with** its replacement call; the cssom cure **with** its fixture **and** the `:28-33` comment correction (**this wave's declared commit-family rule**, §Carry F2: a comment stating an invariant about a mechanism this wave changes is corrected or made executable in the cure commit, never left standing beside a changed mechanism — the previously cited "kf-SquareScene law" was fabricated and is struck, KF-W2-CHECK D-1); the ingress census + the §F-2 addendum; the entry-point contract + its conformance test.

**Packet homing (NO-WAVE-OWNER, homed as authoring requires):** the **square** packet's **parse arm** is HOMED HERE **as a census cell and a contract consumer only** — `useSquareTumble.ts:22` `parseCssColor` (lane-library §4.6's named R1 crash surface) and `useSquareDemo.ts:82` `parseCssScalar`.
**Precision correction at repair round 2 (KF-W2-CHECK D-9; re-executed against the installed 4.0.0 this round, output stated).** The round-1 sentence read *"it throws on `oklch()`/`calc()`"* — an **unqualified reachability claim standing beside a pair-reading lock whose whole content is that reachability claims must be qualified.** Only the **empty-argument** form throws:

```
parseCssColor("oklch()")           → THROW TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')
parseCssColor("calc()")            → THROW TypeError (same)
parseCssColor("calc(1px + 2px)")   → ok:false [css_syntax]        ← NOT a throw
parseCssScalar("calc(1px + 2px)")  → ok:false [css_syntax]        ← the banked D-27 "→ ERR" reading, confirmed as a Result, not a throw
```

**Restated**: at this seam `parseCssColor` **throws on the empty-argument forms** `oklch()` / `calc()` / `rgb()`, and **returns `ok:false [css_syntax]` on well-formed-but-unsupported input** — `calc(1px + 2px)`, `color-mix(in srgb, …)` — and `ok:false [color_context_required]` on `var(--x)`. The distinction is load-bearing in both directions: it is what makes **K-6's token guard** true (no CSS token can resolve to empty-args), and it is what leaves **kf-SquareScene D-27/L-7/C-9**'s authored-CSS path intact (the `!ok → throw` at `useSquareTumble.ts:23` converts an `ok:false` into a throw *in kf's own code*, which is the crash surface — a fact the collapsed sentence erased). Both readings survive **only** when the form is named. **The cure is the square packet's, whose owning wave is KF.W11** (canonical 17-packet roster, RULINGS R-15) — this wave **declares the seam and does not cure it**.

**The square record's own parse rows travel with the seam (repair round 1; KF-W2-CHECK D-2/D-6). Homing a packet's parse arm while dropping the record's parse findings is how a wave inherits a reachability claim the bank already qualified.** Three banked facts ride the `useSquareTumble.ts:22` cell and are stated wherever it is:

1. **K-6's reachability guard** ⟨kf-SquareInstrument:24/:100⟩ — *"R1's true input is empty-args `oklch()` (throws in my run) **which no token can produce** (`getPropertyValue` → `""` → the `if (value)` guard). Safe regardless of cascade order."* **A bare "it throws on `oklch()`" is not a statement of this seam's reachability** and must not be written as one.
2. **The pair reading (RULINGS R-7a, binding)** — K-6 kills exactly one claim, **R1-through-a-token**. It does **not** kill the authored-CSS/`!ok`-throw path, which is established by kf-SquareScene **D-27/L-7/C-9** (*"`calc()` is ordinary authored CSS; `parseCssScalar("calc(1px + 2px)")` → ERR → `num()` throws"*, five throw sites bricking the rAF loop) and by kf-EditorShell **C-19**'s RR-2. **Token path and authored-CSS path are read as a pair**: C-4 governs the token→R1 path, C-5 governs the authored-CSS/throw path, and neither is asserted through the other.
3. **The colour arm's stake is bounded by kf-SquareScene C-1** — *"the colour half of the scene's value.js consumption is **dead paint**"* (the sole colour write is occluded by an opaque `background-image`). So the tumble sweep's *visible* payoff is currently zero while its *crash* exposure is total, and kf-SquareScene **MISS-4**'s per-frame re-parse is what moves the throw *into* the frame path. The census records all three; the cure — degrade-not-throw in the frame path, plus the library rider on `RAFPlayback._run` — is **KF.W11's**, with the engine-seam half routed as the bank routes it.

  **AND THE ONE MISSING ID-LEVEL CLAUSE — `KF-CO-10` ⟨kf-ChannelOptions `:62`⟩, written at repair round 5 (RULINGS-5 §END · KF-W2 `D-4`).** §Carry F0's **Z6** disposed of this id to *"§Excluded's per-cell rule"* — **a clause that did not exist in this file.** It exists now, and it is a read reason, not a blanket. **D-10 CURED — the §Carry-F5 contradiction, in this spec's own hand.** The struck line listed **kf-SequenceScrubber** among *"13 carry no routed cell"* and concluded *"there is no routed cell to own"* — while §Carry F5 **sources the standing KF-AV-28 rider this spec carries** from that record (*"Banked at `kf-AnimationVisualizer.md:35`, `kf-PlaybackRibbon.md:36`, **`kf-SequenceScrubber.md:36`**"*), verified: `kf-SequenceScrubber.md:36` = *"KF-AV-28's standing rider applies: the KF.W7 ScrubberTimeline evaluation gates the component-shape rows."* Citing a record as a bank source for a lock and then declaring it contributes nothing is a self-contradiction — **and Y1/Y6/Y8 compound the same line from the other direction.** The cure is the distinction now written into the table: **a record relates to this register in three separable ways — it may carry a routed cell (adopt it), be a bank source for a carried lock or rider (cite it, no cell), or carry neither (exclude it on a read reason).** kf-SequenceScrubber is the **second**; the round-2 line had only the first and third and forced it into the wrong one.

*Authored as KF.W2's spec **from the 58-record adjudicated registry directly** — the drafting seat's "authored against the CARRY ledger whole" is **STRUCK** (RULINGS **R-12**: the only in-tree carry is `KF-W6-CARRY.md`, 201 rows / 54 records, and it is not this wave's); the fold seat (M-23) verified the drafting seat's product row-for-row and applied three fold repairs (G-W2-2 symbol-scoping, the `coerceToSyntax` cell, the `keyframe_selector_invalid` code). **Repair round 1 (2026-08-28)** then executed RULINGS **R-1b · R-10 · R-12 · R-16** verbatim and cured **D-1..D-10**: 19 escaped registry cells adopted by banked id (anti-rename), the fabricated "kf-SquareScene law" struck and the obligation re-founded, K-6's bank statement corrected with its reachability guard carried, G-W2-1 restated at an enumerated floor of 11, the KF-AV-28 rider carried verbatim with its one governed row named, the phantom ledger struck at every site, and OP-6/G-W2-8 re-declared as an order (KF.W8 precedes). The R1 witness and the entry-point contract witness were **executed read-only by the fold seat** on 2026-08-28 against the installed value.js 4.0.0, discharging OP-3 and re-scoping the claim it was meant to protect.
